## Per

Pers were a warrior race of outsiders that served the various deities of the Upper Planes, primarily by acting as guardians of portals to those planes and of significant junctures of Yggdrasil. They were created by deities from the spirits of humans who in life had been dedicated to a cause and many considered it a great honor to become one.

Description
Pers resembled human fighters with muscular builds, standing  on average. They were typically garbed in elaborately decorated bronze or steel armor and large helmets that were adorned were large plumes or other sorts of ornamentation. Their overall appearance was rather grim and foreboding.

Personality
Pers were rather intelligent beings, but also fanatical in their duties and would never leave their assigned post. If ever challenged, they would fight to the death. Some mistook their dutiful nature for being emotionlessness.

Abilities
All pers possessed abilities that mimicked the spells blade barrier, charm person or mammal which could be cast seven times per day, cure serious wounds which could be cast three times per day, ESP, light, mirror image, and read magic. Of these powers, ESP in particular was frequently used by them as part of their duties.

Pers could not be harmed by any mundane weaponry, they viewed the world with truesight, were difficult to surprise, and they could magically determine an opponent's fighting prowess. Due to the special nature of their existence, all pers possessed a limited mental link with the deities they served, making the power aware whenever a per was in duress or outright killed.

Combat
Each per carried a special sentient two-handed sword, imbued with the essence of a servitor of an Upper Planes deity. These swords were aligned towards lawful neutral, could speak Common, and were always in agreement with their per wielder, though would come into conflict with any other being that tried to wield them. In addition to carrying both a +3 enchantment and an effect similar to a frostbrand, they also had spell-like abilities that mimicked confusion, detect evil, detect good, detect invisible objects, and detect magic. The ability to detect good and evil aligned beings was frequently utilized by the per in their duties.

Society
New pers were created by deities whenever their numbers were running low or whenever a new portal to the Upper Planes was created.
Homelands
Pers could be found throughout much of the Upper Planes, such as in Elysium, various roads on Mount Olympus, and at some of the most important junctions on Yggdrasil. In the Krigala layer of the Beastlands, a number of pers guarded color pools that formed in and around where Yggdrasil's branches reached into the plane.
Languages
All pers spoke common, but also retained the languages of the spirit they were created from.
Relationships
A wide variety of deities across different pantheons worshipped on the world of Toril were known to have pers as servants.
Faerûnian pantheon Within the major pantheon of Faerûn, pers were used as servants by Kelemvor, the Red Knight, and Savras.
Lords of the Golden Hills Of the gnomish deities, Gaerdal Ironhand was the sole member to utilize pers.
Morndinsamman Berronar Truesilver, Clangeddin Silverbeard, Gorm Gulthyn, and Moradin.
Seldarine Corellon and Solonor Thelandira.
Yondalla's Children Cyrrollalee and Yondalla.

Among the Olympian pantheon, a number of pers acted as lesser proxies for the deity Apollo, each entrusted with minor tasks.

Notable Pers
 Erik Boldswift, a servant of the Norse deity Heimdall that was rumored to guard a portal in the mountain forests of Krigala that lead to Ysgard.

## Periscot

Periscot was a halfling thief and cook in the late 14 century DR who died while on an expedition in the Shining Sea.

History
In the, sometime around the month of Flamerule, a group of eccentric entertainers, entrepreneurs, and scientists embarked upon a colonizing expedition to an island they discovered in the Shining Sea off the coast of Chult, which they dubbed the "Forgotten Island". Periscot was among the members of this group, alongside her thief husband Jarkus. Led by a charismatic Waukeenar priest named Devlin, the group sought to create a tourist attraction on the isle that would attract wealthy people from all across the Realms. Three months later, things began to go awry for the expedition.

On Marpenoth 5, two members of the group by the names of Argus and Wedonai were patrolling the island and came across the horrifically mutilated corpse of a stegosaurus. Wedonai would go on to express concerns about angered forest spirits, whilst Argus would tell Devlin that the creature's wounds suggested an attack from another dinosaur. Instead of letting the issue trouble his mind, Devlin decided to spent much of the day instructing Periscot in the kitchen, since their potential investor Prince Faruq of Calimport had a particular palate.

On Marpenoth 11, five velociraptors attacked the expedition's campsite and the team managed to slay them, but not without Argus dying and the fighter Mallus getting injured. The next day Periscot's husband Jarkus left with Wedonai and the mage Briella Farthing to check on the Sacred Heart of the Beast, an ancient Sunite relic the expedition had brought with them that was supposed to have made docile all of the animals on the island.  Sailing off the island began to be considered, but could not be pursued as heavy storms kept everyone else in the campsite for Marpenoth 13 and 14. When the storms finally let up on Marpenoth 16, the group went on to the docks and discovered their ships were wrecked, thus stranding them on the island.

Over the course of the next three days, the group became infected with olive slimes due to close proximity with Mallus, who had contracted an infection from the velociraptors. On the night of the third day, Marpenoth 19, Devlin snuck into the barracks and murdered everyone inside. Finding bits of olive slime on all of their bodies, Devlin worked to scrape the ooze off them. He burned the scrapings in a wicker basket and then proceeded to burn the bodies of everyone inside the barracks. Before hanging himself the next day, Periscot's name and the names of the rest of the expedition crew would be recorded by Devlin in the last entry of his diary.

Two days later, Marpenoth 22, a group of adventurers arrived to the island in search of Briella Farthing and investigated the ruined campsite. There they discovered the badly burnt corpse of Periscot still lying within the ruins of the camp's barracks.

Penelope's book – Adopters of Culture, was an anthropological work that voiced her opinion on the culture, or lack thereof, among the half-orcish people of Faerûn. The book was published sometime in or before the  and found among the possessions of the inhabitants of Baldur's Gate.

Penelope's cousin, Joyce Ringwarble, did not share Penelope's favorable view of half-orcs. She showed disdain and desire to sterilize them in her work titled Tusky Grins. Joyce believed this event was the catalyst for Penelope's descent into madness.

## Peela Crivens

Peela Crivens was one of the inhabitants of Reithwin, the town that once venerated Selûne in the Western Heartlands, sometime before the late 14 century DR.

History
Peela Crivens perished in a tragic event sometime before the fall of Reithwin, before the, and before it was consumed by the Shadow-cursed Lands. The event took the lives of Peela, Brent, and almost every member of the Crivens clan, including the family dog – Patch, outlived only by Errick Crivens, who was, in some way, the cause of the tragedy. Peela was buried in Reithwin's graveyard behind the House of Healing in a cluster surrounded by the graves of her family. Her tombstone read: "Dedicated to Peela Crivens, taken along with her family on the same grim night. May their deaths be avenged." The Crivens family's grave markers remained relatively undamaged and legible as of the .

## Peqqir

Peqqir was a city in the Plain of Horses of northeastern Kara-Tur. It was the home of the Guychiang tribe.

Geography
Peqqir stood in the taiga of the northern Plain of Horses, near the foot of the Chigidi Mountains and with the Anai River flowing past. A road following the river linked it to Mount Or-Ghash in the west and the other cities of the Plain of Horses in the east. Peqqir was surrounded by green forests and rolling hills, and enjoyed some of the most fertile farmland in the region.

Trade
Expert farmers and employing advanced irrigation methods, the Guychiang grew grains, vegetables, cotton, and mulberry trees, the leaves of which were used in the production of silk. In addition, they mined iron and jade from the nearby Chigidi Mountains and craftsmen manufactured weapons, quality jewelry, and leather products. All in all, Peqqir was perhaps the most industrious settlement in all the Plain of Horses as of .

Defenses
Defense of their city was paramount to the Guychiang. To enter Peqqir, a traveler must pass through a succession of sibege, heavily fortified gates in the city wall. A squad of well-armed soldiers stood guard on each sibege. A permit issued by city officials and payment of a gate tax were required just to pass through one sibege.

History
After he first appeared in the Plain of Horses around, the nomad lama Torgoja P'a stopped in Peqqir, where he aided in the design of an irrigation project that would bring water to mulberry farms on mountain slopes. The grateful inhabitants offered to construct a temple in his honor, but P'a passed on it and continued on his way. He would become High Lama of Mount Or-Ghash around .

## Penita

Penita was a sailor aboard the Lustrous Lass and sister to a cleric of the Open Hand Temple, Assuagia, in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Penita was a rock gnome woman with a clean-shaven head.

History
In the, Captain Harfang sailed the Lustrous Lass to the Crone Island, where she made a deal with the Gloom Crone. Captain Harfang was granted "enough gold to last the rest of her life," but soon enough, the Captain realized that the bag of gold contained only six real gold pieces among the dross. As soon as she tossed the false gold aboard, Harfang came down with deadly sea plague that ended the lives of the entire crew in a bit over a tenday after the deal was made.

The bodies of the dead, including Penita's, were left aboard the Lustrous Lass and quarantined after she drifted into the harbors of Baldur's Gate.

## Peanut

Peanuts, also known as groundnuts, were a type of legume that produced edible shelled underground nuts that could be pulverized to make spreadable and edible peanut butter.

Usages
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue offered peanuts for sale in its exotics section, priced at 30 gold coins per pound.

A simple dessert treat called Monkey Balls included peanut butter and could be found in Amn. A copy of the recipe was kept in Dradeel's recipe collection in Spellhold. Further south, peanuts were eaten in Calimshan, where they were baked and eaten as a side dish.

In the Vast, peanuts could be found in Ravens Bluff's Downunda Patisserie. Its Anniversary Specials menu included a box of chocolate-covered peanut and almond clusters.

Inhabitants of Port Nyanzaru in Chult consumed a dish of fried kapenta fish, cooked with onions and groundnut powder.

Lastly, peanuts were found growing and consumed in Kara-Tur.

History
Peanuts, or groundnuts, were eaten as early as the ancient empire of Netheril, where more primitive tribes foraged for peanuts to stew them.

## Pelops

Pelops, nicknamed Shadowstriker, was a halfling thief and adventurer and a member of the Company of the Sundered Orc in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

History
Five adventurers—Lorar, Pelops, Jared Rheloph, Cassandra Rulmac, and Wynara Rulmac—came together in Daggerford relatively recently as of 1368 DR and soon headed for Waterdeep, not yet even having a name for their company. En route, they encountered a small orc raiding band and destroyed them, so they named themselves for this event.

In Waterdeep, they went to the Yawning Portal inn and began exploring the dungeons of Undermountain as adventurers do. In the, they elected to locate a supposed treasure hoard on the second level, the Arcane Chambers, which the Waterdhavian merchant-adventurer Essimuth Lanys claimed to have sighted. They ventured down the well just after sunset on Eleint 2, and had not yet returned by the end of the month. The Tymoran priest Adama Miiralin stationed at the inn recorded this in his monthly report to High Priestess Seenroas Halvinhar.

In fact, all five of them had been captured by drow slavers in the Arcane Chambers and were soon to be transported to Skullport.

## Pentekonter

Pentekonters were a type galleyWithin the supplement Of Ships and the Sea (1997), penekonters are described as being a type of bireme, but historically they were monoremes (a ship with a single row of oars) and lacked any such hollowed outriggers. This misinformation is carried over in the description of a pentekonter in the novel Rising Tide (1999). that could be found in the Realms.

Description
On average, from bow to stern these ships measured  in length, had a beam (width) of, and required a depth of at least  in order to float. They had large, square sails and were always outfitted with a sharply pointed ram, just below the surface of the water. Their prows were rounded, making them look sluggish, but they were deceptively fast ships.

These ships had two banks of oars, which enabled greater speeds to be achieved when necessary. The second bank of oars was stationed in hollowed outriggers attached to the hull, a level below the raised deck, which helped pentekonters to maintain stability. They held enough space to carry up to  of cargo, but lacked any specialized quarters for passengers.

Each pentekonter could be manned by a crew of sixty individuals, all rowers, but could easily fit an extra fifty warrior passengers in place of cargo. The bare minimum of rowers necessary to sail a pentekonter was twenty, with the other forty acting as a skeleton crew.

History
On Ches 30, in the, as part of the Twelfth Serôs War, Iakhovas sneaked a horde of his sahuagin and wererats into Waterdeep Harbor by means of a modified penekonter to enact a surprise raid. The ship had a hole made in its hull, allowing its sahuagin crew to submerge themselves in the water every four hours. Whenever this occurred someone else had to take the rower's place, as they had to be submerged for an equal amount of time before they returned to full strength. To compensate for this hole in the hull, the hollowed outriggers were modified. This consequently caused the ship to be submerged within the water at a lower depth than a standard pentekonter.

## Pea

Pea was a variety of edible legume grown on delicate vine plants with white flowers, harvested from elongated pods and used for food by humanoids and centaurs, among other creatures of the Realms. Several species of peas existed, including split, green and yellow.

Availability
In the Waterdhavian Bowels of the Earth tavern, peas were served stewed in a thick concoction of lentils, fat, bones, and meat scraps. This use was common across Faerûn; in other places, these "everything in" stews were thickened with mashed yellow peas. Aurora's Emporium sold peas and green peas for 2 gp per pound. In the city of Baldur's Gate and the Chionthar Valley, roast beef joint dish was garnished with peas. Split pea soup, in the style of the Dalelands, was flavored with smoked ham and sprinkled with a generous amount of pepper, commonly served in the region's eateries.

In the Forest Kingdom of Cormyr, peas were cultivated in the town of Immersea. West of Cormyr, peas were grown in a small village of stone huts in the Veilstone Peaks – Bedford, where such crops as peas and potatoes could grow in rocky terrain.

In the Vilhon Reach, peas were grown in parts of the nation of Turmish.

In Calimshan and the Calim Empire, peas were among common dried ration foods, alongside figs, prunes, flat bread, dates, and goat cheese.

In the Vast, peas were a product of the Cottswald family, grown in the fields by the family's manor outside of Ravens Bluff. North of Ravens Bluff, one could purchase a hearty mean at the Hog Hill. Such meals included stewed pork, goat, or chicken with carrots, lentils, peas, parsnips, onions, and squashes.

Further northeast, barley and peas were grown in abundance in Ra-Khati country in the Hordelands. And in the Utter East, peas were cultivated in the hidden country of Langdarma within the Yehimal Mountains.

In Zakhara, peas could be found stored in the granaries and kitchens of Sarahin in the Haunted Lands.

Usage
The Savage Frontier, and the lands just south of Neverwinter served a staple pea soup, scooped into cut open bread loaves.

In the Dalelands and in Shadowdale, peas were commonly eaten with venison and potatoes – a strange combination for people of Turmish.

Peas were commonly dried for storage and to be consumed. Dried peas were also used by many thieves to scatter in front of pursuers like marbles or ball bearings. This practice went as far back as the ancient Athalantar. Dried peas in the north were exported out of Calimshan and Tashalar.

Trick locks – carved wooden balls used by gnomes and dwarves, hid hollow compartments filled with dried peas or metal chimes, allowing the items to rattle making them good toys for the children.

In magic, dried peas were used as a spell component to cast the fog cloud spell. Powdered dried peas, in turn, were used to cast the solid fog, death fog, and wall of fog spells. Priests of Lliira could conjure the triple mask spell with three peas as the material components.

History
In the, three Chosen of Silvanus got together on Sapra with the leaders of the Turmishan druidic order known as the Emerald Enclave and conducted a magical ritual that ended a famine that had been plaguing that nation. This caused all manner of food to instantaneously grow throughout the land, including peas.

## Pegasus language

The Pegasus language was the racial language of pegasi.

Speakers
Beyond pegasi themselves, Pegasus was a common secondary language among many members of Faerûn's land dwelling elven subraces, such as the lythari, moon elves, sun elves, and wood elves.

Pegasus was also fairly common as a secondary language among druids devoted to certain nature deities. These included the druids of Chauntea, druids of Eldath, druids and druid/rangers of Mielikki, and the druids of Silvanus. Other forms of specialty priests in the Realms for whom Pegasus was a commonly known secondary language included the Eldathyn peacemen.

## Peg

Wooden pegs were simple fasteners that were used in carpentry and construction. They belonged to a broad class of hardware that included bolts, nails, and spikes.

Description
Pegs were similar to wooden stakes, but were smaller and not intended for camping or other rough uses. They were cut with one pointed end and one flat end.

Availability
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue offered a selection of pegs and related tools in various configurations and sizes. Pegs were available and cut to length from long dowels in ½" (5 cp/foot),  (1 sp/foot), and  (3 sp/foot) diameters.

Usage
Wooden pegs and nails were generally less popular than metal nails and spikes, as they had shorter functional lifespans and were more susceptible to weathering.

Nails and pegs were commonly used for ship construction in Faerûn, while in Zakhara this was avoided, with ship timbers instead sewn together with coconut fiber rope. This produced lighter ships that better handled reefs and shoals, and allowed them to survive the influence of a great lodestone deposit beneath the Crowded Sea.

In the ancient Netherese city of Scourge, the region's unnatural rusting winds meant that nails (and any other metal fittings, tools, and weapons) were useless. Instead, wooden nails were used for construction.

## Pearlspore bell

Pearlspore bells were a species of huge mundane mushrooms that could be imbued with haggish fell magics, found sometime growing in the Western Heartlands.

Usages
Pearlspore bells was known to be used by hags to avoid being slain. A hag could link herself with one of several clusters of pearlspore bells and, eventually, be reborn through the fungi after being slain. This was believed to be a speculation by some.

History
The book called Fastidious Fungi included some information on hags and the pearlspore bell mushrooms that could be used to return a slain hag to the land of the living. In the, a copy of Fastidious Fungi was studied by Mayrina after forming the Anti-Hag Support Group, in search of ways to stop hags' sinister and malicious plots. The same year, green hag Auntie Ethel imbued several pearlspore bells with her magics in attempts to keep herself alive. The mushrooms were planted in her subterranean lair under the Blushing Maiden Inn.

## Peasant Dynasty

Peasant Dynasty was a common variety of enchanted kama.

Description
It was richly decorated and fit for courtiers and not work in the fields.

Powers
Each of these kama bore a simple +1 enchantment that guided the weapon towards the target and slightly increased its damage, as well as made it possible to harm creatures typically unaffected by mundane weapons. Peasant Dynasty also granted the wielder a small degree of resistance to acid, cold, lightning, and fire.

History
The history behind the creation of these kama was undiscovered; however, their design led some sages to speculate that despite their markings and appearance being similar to courtly daggers wielded by nobles and royals, the original Peasant Dynasty was created or commissioned by a courtier who rose up from the lowly life of field workers and peasants—an unusual thing that was outside the traditions of royal bloodlines.

By the late 14 century DR, some of these enchanted kama or copies made from the originals were found in the city of Neverwinter and nearby lands.

## Peasant Blade

Peasant Blades were enchanted kama created to be used as weapons rather than tools of the trade.

Powers
Peasant Blade was enhanced with a simple +3 enchantment that made its slashes harmful even to creatures unaffected by mundane weapons and guided the wielder's hand towards the target. Additionally, the weapon significantly boosted the wielder's constitution.

History
Unsurprisingly, Peasant Blades were once tools used by peasants in the fields, later adapted to be used in battle because they were forbidden from wielding swords. By the late 14 century DR, some of these enchanted kama or copies made from the original, were found in the city of Neverwinter and nearby lands.

Description
Petric Amber was a wood elven man with sharp facial features and a skin tone tinted with floral green-brown. He had long brown hair and wore a monocle over his right eye. He preferred blue finery and wore a resplendent hat decorated with a single massive feather.

History
In the, shortly before the armies of the Cult of the Absolute attacked the city of Baldur's Gate, Lord Petric Amber was a Parliamentarian of the Gate who voted in support of crowning lowborn Lord Enver Gortash as the city's Archduke. Lord Amber was present for Gortash's coronation that took place in the audience hall of Wyrm's Rock. Following the coronation, Petric stated that he would support the new Archduke as long as Gortash honored the agreement between them.

## Petras

Pale Petras was a vampire spawn and one of the favorite "children" of the vampire lord Cazador Szarr, who resided in the Szarr Palace of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Petras was a pale human male with crimson eyes that glowed with the typical sinister vampire's gaze. Like his "siblings," Petras's back was carved with infernal runes.

History
Like his "siblings," the purpose of Petras's existence was a powerful ritual to be performed by Cazador Szarr in the . As a result, the vampire spawns were to be destroyed, and their power would transform Cazador into a vampire ascendant. Petras was known to hunt alongside his "sister," Dalyria, and during one of these hunts, the two might had came across the prodigal "sibling," Astarion Ancunín and the Absolute Adversaries, as they reached the city of Baldur's Gate. Eventually, all of Cazador's spawn were needed for the ritual to succeed; however, it was unknown if the Black Mass was successful and if Petras was destroyed, or if the Black Mass never was completed, leaving Petras freed from his dark master, or if he was slain in the process by the Absolute Adversaries and Astarion.

## Perils We Should Beware

Perils We Should Beware was a chapbook released anonymously during the Spellplague era that discussed various types of threats that plagued Faerûn. Its author was unknown to the public, having chosen to remain anonymous.

Reputation
Perils We Should Beware was most notable for coining the term "Slithering Cults" – that being, cults dedicated to the worship of oozes and slimes – which it asserted was chief amongst the threats facing Faerûn at that time. It was widely circulated along trade routes during the Spellplague era, leading to the term "Slithering Cults" to enter common Faerûnian parlance.

## Peron Uthe

Peron Uthe was a famed ranger of Eveningstar and the King's Forest in Cormyr during the mid-1480s DR.

History

In the late 15 century DR, Peron was a renowned and beloved resident of Eveningstar. He lived in the village at Uthe Lodge, and traveled widely about the region, including to the King's Forest. When the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar commenced, he disregarded the drow threat and continued as normal as he roamed and performed his duties.

Around this time, Peron visited the druidic hideaway Thorngap and was infected with a magical disease inflicted by the malevolent druid Halsaime. He returned from the King's Forest to his home Uthe Lodge, then to the Eveningstar War Hospital because he felt unwell. A short while later he transformed into a red musk zombie with large red musk creeper flowers growing from him and began spreading his strange disease to the rest of the hospital, though while others could be turned into red musk zombies, they could not themselves pass on the disease to more victims. In addition to the zombies, primal creatures like mudmen, wisps, and vine horrors began infiltrating the hospital and unnaturally verdant plants began blooming and overgrowing the structure, much like in his home.

When the hospital staff noticed what was happening, they barricaded Peron Uthe into a wing of the hospital and sought reinforcements. The priestess Meena Follakin found adventurers who were able to help, and sent them on to the head priest Hurim Jaskyr who explained the situation. The team rescued and cured as many patients as they could while fighting off the zombies, gathering samples from the fallen Mikhael Jarvin and Aeron Leifbrand as they went. Jaskyr used these samples to create an inoculation against Peron's disease, allowing the heroes to face him directly. Though he had grown into a huge and strong plant-thing, they struck what was left of him down, ending his rampage. Without Peron around the red musk plague in Eveningstar was brought to a halt, though his home had become dominated by the murderous dryad Sylvie and her minions, which was spotted and reported on by Eolynn Arva.

## Perfect Fang of Lolth

The Perfect Fang of Lolth was a unique enchanted scythe, part of a set alongside the Perfect Charm of Lolth, forged deep in the Demonweb Pits, the Abyssal layer ruled by Lolth, The Demon Queen of Spiders.

Description
The Perfect Fang was an ornate weapon, carved with ancient magical runes of the Demonweb Pits.

Powers
This powerful Lolthite scythe was built to rip into the souls of the enemies of the Spider Queen. The weapon's powers increased when it was wielded alongside the Perfect Charm of Lolth. In addition to dramatically increased damage health by the scythe, it healed the wielder with each successful slash. The Perfect Fang also magically shielded its wielder from incoming damage when their stamina was exhausted during battle. Lastly, at the beginning of each battle, the scythe increased the wielder's chance of dealing increased damage with a lucky or well-aimed attack.

The weapon's magic made it usable by barbarians only.

History
The Perfect Fang of Lolth and the Perfect Charm of Lolth were forged in eons past deep in the Demonweb Pits. The weapons found their way to the Prime Material plane in the late 15 century DR, possibly ending up in the hands of drow and adventurers of the Northdark who challenged the beholder deity Gzemnid, or barbarians who survived a descent into the Demonweb Pits and a battle with Lolth herself.

## Pestkiller

A pestkillerDemihumans of the Realms states in its introduction that the "pestkiller" kit was partially a fusion of the "pest controller" and "vermin slayer" kits from The Complete Book of Dwarves. It also lists many skill proficiencies that were introduced in The Complete Book of Dwarves. was a professional expert in combating all manner of creatures considered vermin.

Activities
The standard priorities of any pestkiller on the job were identifying vermin and eliminating them, often through the use of traps. They spent considerable time studying vermin in order to design effective traps—live specimens were usually preferred, as traps designed based on the study of dead specimens were only half as effective. The study of live specimens was also necessary in order to devise effective poison powders. Some pestkillers were known to develop a peculiar sort of empathy and understanding towards certain vermin after extensive studying, though this never kept them from eliminating the pest.

For pests of small size and base intelligence, pestkillers designed and baited specialized traps for around 1 to 4 gold pieces. For larger vermin, pestkillers used wire snares and bear traps, typically concealed and clamped to the ground by means of a chain or spike. Setting up such traps usually took around 1 to 3 days and cost 10 gp. Other potential forms of traps included such things as drop nets, small deadfalls, spring traps, and iron doors to close off individual sections of a tunnel.

The typical animals considered "vermin" that pestkillers fought against ranged from all manner of arthropods (like centipedes and millipedes), arachnids (scorpions and spiders), bats, small lizards, rats, snakes, weasels and giant weasels. They also were known to combat more monstrous creatures and certain pestering humanoids, such as carrion crawlers, jermlaines, kobolds, mites, rot grubs, all types of fungi and oozes, and other ameboid creatures.

Culture
The life of a pestkiller was typically taken up by the small, industrious demihuman races of Faerûn, namely hairfeet, rock gnomes, shield dwarves, and stout halflings.

By the 14 century DR, pestkillers had been a staple of many large cities all across Faerûn, such as Calimport and Neverwinter. They were also common in the cities along the Sea of Fallen Stars, as ships had a tendency to track rats into their port-cities. Beneath the surface, dwarf and gnome strongholds relied upon pestkillers yearly to combat the creatures of the Underdark, as well as any vermin that stowed away in their food stores or trade goods. Their expertise in rigging in traps could also prove valuable if a stronghold fell under attack.

Their line of work inevitably lead pestkillers to become experts in navigating city sewers and urban dungeons, or the varied tunnels and passageways of the stronghold they inhabited.

While most were content with their profession, some pestkillers were enamored by the excitement of adventuring. Such individuals would aid adventuring companies in delving into vermin-infested dungeons or act as guides for those heading into subterranean areas in which they lived.

Despite the essential work pestkillers provided society, few people were truly aware of the vast knowledge that they had. They were typically considered lower class, with few having any respect for them. This could even be the case among dwarves, as shield dwarf pestkillers often trimmed their beards short, which in turn made them perceived as having a lower status in the eyes of their fellow dwarves. Pestkillers typically lived as loners, due to their lack of hygiene and unkempt appearance putting people off, as well as their  tendency to be carriers of disease.

Abilities
A pestkiller was typically defined by their proficiency in capturing underground pests with traps, blacksmithing (for the manufacturing of traps), herbalism (for the manufacturing of poisons), and a deep knowledge of the habits of their animal foes. Other skills commonly found among pestkillers included expertise in recognizing fungi, the ability to imitate the calls and noises of various animals, hunting, tracking, building fires, and the ability to enter a deep trance to slow their respiration.

Pestkillers were skilled in navigating and fighting in the constricted passageways and tunnels created by many kinds of vermin.

Beyond their varied repertoire of skills, pestkillers were noted for building up an immunity to the various natural poisons and venoms produced by the monsters, insects, reptiles, snakes, spiders, and other vermin that they regularly faced. However, this tolerance did not extend to manufactured poisons, such as those that were commonly used by assassins. Their extended exposure to the unsanitary environments vermin were found in also made pestkillers uniquely resilient towards many common infections, parasites, and non-magical diseases.

Possessions
The outfit of a pestkiller was often dirty and odorous, but made of durable material. Boots were considered a necessity, gloves and hand wrappings were common, and extra padding was often added to their elbows and knees to cushion them when crawling. Bandanas were a common accessory among dwarves. Belts and other forms of straps were never used, as they ran the risk of getting snagged in the narrow environs that pestkillers frequented.

If armor was ever needed, pestkillers would opt for something light, allowing them a granter range of movement. If their armor was loose, a baggy tunic would be worn over top to mitigate the chance of it snagging on projections in narrow passages and to muffle noise. Armor would be paired with a thick leather helmet, fastened down over the head, and occasionally outfitted with a metal plate to guard against attacks in crawlspaces.

In terms of weaponry, pestkillers utilized many of the same tools as rogues, as well as blowguns, hand axes, short bows, warhammers, and light crossbows. Such weapons were preferred as they could be wielded effectively in confined spaces. They also were known to manufacture poison powders of their own design, tailored to a specific vermin. These were most effective against "cleanup crew" monsters, like green slimes and rot grubs. Making this powder took 3 to 12 days and cost around 10 to 100 gp in supplies that could easily be acquired within any large city in the Realms.

Combat
In contrast to those who took up the life of a giant-killer, their choice of study left pestkillers deficient in combating giants, giant-kin, ogres, ogre magi, and trolls.

## Peral

Peral was a clinging vine plant and a weed hated by farmers and gardeners of Cormyr.

Description
Peral was a fast-growing vine that enveloped and choked other plants to death. The vine was so dangerous to the plant life that only trees thicker than  in diameter could survive. Despite being a weed, peral sap was useful. It was sticky and sweet and was used in cooking in a similar manner to honey and as a type of adhesive. The sap was strong enough to keep paper envelopes sealed and similar small items. However, it crystalized and crumbled to dust within a week.

Peral sap was expensive as the vines were difficult to cultivate. They produced sap only when grown near other plants and allowed to choke them and only after three seasons of sprouting and strangulations.

## Peppercorn

Peppercorns or black pepper, was a spice, a dried berry, harvested from a peppercorn plant vine, a staple in cooking across Faerûn.

Description
Peppercorns grew in small berry clusters and there existed a variety of colors.

Availability
Black pepper was a common table spice in the Dalelands. The dish called stuffed turkey pie at The Watchful Eye in Essembra, Battledale, used black pepper as a seasoning; Old Tower Fowl at The Rising Moon in Highmoon, Deepingdale called for a handful of black pepper; and so did High Dale Lamb served at The Swordsmith's House in Highcastle, the High Dale.

Peppercorns were available in the Blade Kingdoms of the Vilhon Reach, where they were used in establishing of "peppercorn democracy," a legal way of giving voice to all citizens of the Kingdoms, and not just the Blade Captains. The political movement adopted banners emblazoned with peppercorns.

Usages
In Waterdeep, black pepper was used to season Dragon Soup prepared by Feldyn Goadolfyn of The Old Monster Shop. While in Baldur's Gate, black pepper and nutmeg were commonly used to season mashed potatoes. And Neverwintran prime rib steak was made with a sauce of peppercorns and wine.

The Old Wheel club in the City of Caravans, Crimmor, of Amn, served smoked Athkatlan harpfish, encrusted in sweet sauce and cracked black peppercorns. Further south, in the land of Tethyr, pepper cheese was made with peppercorns. This cheese was sold across the realms via Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue.

The simple eatery of The Last Jack in the city of Ravens Bluff, served subpar selection of dishes, including bread with peppercorn butter.

Peppercorns were used by the inhabitants of the Malatran Plateau of Kara-Tur and were considered a valuable trade commodity there as part of pickling spices.

## Petyr

Petyr was a traveling priest of Nobanion, who found himself in the Ten-Towns of Icewind Dale in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Petyr was a young and beautiful man with a head of golden red hair. He had a deep and clear voice.

Personality
As a devote follower of Nobanion, Petyr respected his deity as a strong hunter and protector of the wildlife and protector of the week. His animal companion was a gift from the deity – the enormous lioness named Suzy.

History
Petyr hailed from the Vilhon Reach, where he lived surrounded by the followers of Lord Firemane. His animal companion, Suzy, came from the hot plains of Shaar and was a fellow servant of Nobanion. In the, Petyr started receiving visions from his god, compelling him and Suzy to head out thousands of miles north, to the frozen tundras of Icewind Dale, despite of their natural dislike of the cold climate. Suzy possessed a gift of Nobanion that kept her warm and safe in Icewind Dale's low temperatures. Lord Firemane ordered Petyr to protect the wildlife that was just as endangered by the encroaching ancient doom as the humans of the Ten Towns. In his visions, the deity told him of the coming of the famed Hero of Neverwinter whom Petyr was to ally with.

One of Petyr's asks of the Hero of Neverwinter was finding powerful creatures, wild beasts imbued with power by the region's spirit defenders. A group of powerful beasts received such a boon as the ancient evil began threatening Icewind Dale. However, these legendary beasts were driven mad by pain and hunger and twisted by the evil's corrupting influence. Petyr asked the Hero to put these beasts out of their misery. Their locations were unknown but the beasts were known as the master of crags, the master of caves, the master of the tundra, and the last – the master of the sea."

## Peeryst Trumpettower

Peeryst Trumpettower was a nobleman of Hastarl in Athalantar in the 3 century DR.

Description
During his wedding, he wore a high-plumed hat, and a cloth-of-gold doublet. He normally wore bell-trimmed hose and curl-tipped shoes.

Possessions
Peeryst had received for his wedding, from the bride's father, an immense diamond carved into the shape of a stag, which was placed in his bridal chamber. Vigorous activity in the room shattered it to pieces on its first night.

Relationships
He was married to Nanue Glarmeir. His uncle was Darrigo Trumpettower.

History
As part of his marriage to Nanue Glarmeir in the, Peeryst paraded past the temples of Sune, Lathander, Helm, and Tyche, completing the circuit under the sword of Tyr. He also received a statue known as the Stag of Athalantar. However, shortly after the toast at their bridal chamber, the newlyweds were attacked by the Moonclaws gang, and a fight resulted as they clashed with the Velvet Hands gang, which also tried to burgle the couple on their bridal night. Only the intervention of Darrigo Trumpettower finally evicted the thieves, though they caused much damage to their possessions in the meantime.

Later in the, Peeryst Trumpettower was in Athalgard with his wife during the invasion of the forces under Prince Elminster Aumar, who sought to overthrow his uncle Belaur Aumar and stop the Magelords of Athalantar who ruled for him. As Peeryst thought to check what the alarm was about, a soldier ran into him, making him fall and break his nose against the wall. As his wife came to check on him, fellow nobleman Jansibal Otharr, scrambling to his post, injured his wife, cutting into her arm with the sword he had out. His uncle decided to take the fight to Jansibal, took Peryst's blade, and told him to bind Nanue's wounds.

Rumors & Legends
It was said that Darrigo Trumpettower had hurled all of the Moonclaws thieves out of his nephew's bridal suite while the newlyweds consummated without noticing the tumult, which was an embellishment.

## Pecan

Pecans were an exotic variety of nut in the Realms that grew as the fruit of trees of the same name, which were a species of hickory.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold pecans under it's "Aurora's Larder" section's "Exotics" category for a whopping 150 gold pieces per .
 By contrast, in the Vast the shop Fruits And More in the city of Ravens Bluff sold  bags of pecans for 5 gp.
 In interior Faerûn, in the land of Turmish, one could find wild pecan trees growing in the bogs near the Alaoreum River, flourishing where the village of Morningstar Hollows once stood.
 In west Faerûn, in the land of Tethyr. While traveling along the Zazesspur Road one could encounter a round mound of a hill, atop which sprang a lone, mighty pecan tree.
 In Interior Zakhara, pecans were cultivated in the herb garden of the fortress Sarahin.
Beyond Toril
 Pecans could be found in the Feywild.The event referenced on this page by Neifion, of having to choke down sugared plums and toasted pecans in the Feast Never Ending, took place in City of Torment in Darroch Castle.
 Beyond Toril, pecan trees could be found on a world known as Earth. On that planet the tree's nuts were commonly baked into pies or incorporated into ice cream. Both of these desserts were quite popular with the wizard Elminster Aumar.

Usages
 Mraed, a type of nut-based desert, traditionally used pecans.
 Nutbread crescents, a popular snack food in Halruaa, were made with a combination of nuts that traditionally included pecans.
 Roast gammon pie, a Cormyrian dish, called for 3 cups of pecan nuts. The nuts all had to be diced and lightly roasted.

History
To celebrate its sixth anniversary in the, the Downunda Patisserie in Ravens Bluff whipped up a large menu of special dishes. One of these dishes were cinnamon rolls topped with pecans and raisins. The bakery sold these in groups of a half a dozen for just one silver piece.

Trivia
 Beldargan groundworms were said to have a taste akin to roasted pecans.
 The wizard Fannt Golsway owned a staff of thunder & lightning that was crafted of pecan wood.

## Pelloth

Pelloth was a drow mercenary who worked for the infamous Dom in Cormyr during the mid-1480s DR.

Description
Pelloth had long flowing white hair, and wore a suit of plate mail while carrying two scimitars coated in poison.

Relationships
Pelloth had an unknown bond with Dom that ensured his loyalty. While on the surface he befriended and tamed a brush creeper giant spider from the King's Forest, keeping it as a companion.

History

Coming up from the Underdark like Udere, Pelloth took up work as a mercenary and joined Dom's team. His last mission took them to Greenweald Lane, where Dom was hired by the Shadovar of Thultanthar to waylay Bensen Virandson as he transported a fragment of a sought-after Nether Scroll to the nearby Harper Lodge. Unfortunately for Pelloth, his unfamiliarity with the surface world caused his ambush to disturb a hungry dire bear, allowing Bensen and his allies to ambush him right back during the confusion, ending his career.

## Pearled Harp

The Pearled Harp, also called the Purple Pearls, was a trading coster based in the city of Calimport, Calimshan, in the late 14 century DR.

Activities
The coster traded in both Calimport and Cormyr.

History
The coster was formerly owned by Pasha Samiir el Volahrn, who'd grown powerful off the business. However, when he passed away in the late 1360s DR,This is "recently" as of Flamerule in 1370 DR. he had no heirs. Thus, agents of Syl-Pasha Ralan el Pesarkhal went to Suzail, Cormyr, to inform Samiir's daughter by a courtesan, Vanda Volahrn, of her of her father's passing and of her birthright. They offered her Samiir's status as pasha and ownership of the Pearled Harp, provided she used it to support certain trade alliances beneficial to Calimshan's ruler. This made her one of the only female pashas in Calimport and blocked rival merchants who'd coveted her father's coster for themselves, and made her a target for death.

## Penbroc

Penbroc was a tiny kingdom of the Whamite Isles in the Sea of Fallen Stars. It participated in a series of wild conflicts against the other nations and pirates of the Whamite Isles at some point prior to the mass death of the population in the .

Description
Penbroc had a variety of colorful political figures that vied for control of the nation and the Whamite Isles. Known individuals were the kings Finerb, Tmosic III, Ralph MCVI.  Other royalty included the Queen Sweetsia and Prince Ralph MCVII. They were served by the Palace Guard.

Penbroc had a complex structure of nobility - some wealthy, some poor. A third group of nobles were known to be missing, and only revealed their true nature in the heat of conflict. The people of Penbroc, while humble, participated in the wars of the Whamite isles along with the royalty and nobility.

Penbroc shared land borders with Veldergauttland, Bolgor, and Graubünden. The River Gruus seperated Penbroc from Bolgor and Graubunden, while the border with Veldergauttland was a range of mountains. The historic fortress of Gruuscastle stood on the border between Penbroc, Bolgor, and Graubünden, and the three nations often fought over it. This was in contrast to Castle Tmos, which remained firmly within Penbroc's grasp.

Among the nations of the Whamites, Penbroc was unique for its overabundance of kings.

## Permanent gel

Permanent gel was a type of magical hairstyling gel that indefinitely altered the shape of someone's hair.

Powers
When a hairstyle that had been shaped with permanent gel was reshaped in any way, it would immediately reform into its original shape. The permanent gel could not be washed out without powerful intervention. If this happened, or the hair was cut, the removed gel became a slithering tracker after 1 hour. The tracker would then pursue the one who had once had it in their hair, and attempt to once more attach itself to their hairs. If this was impossible, such as in situations when the victim's hair had been signifcantly cut, the tracker would turn violent and strike to kill them.

One of the known ways to rid someone of permanent gel, was to completely destroy their hair by way of fire or acid.

Notable Owners
The annis Errevastica owned a bottle of permanent gel. She kept it in her underground lair beneath Jooge Nopsmoth's barbershop, in Silverymoon.

## Petrove

Petrove was a pirate, and the great-uncle of Coprates Bile in the mid-14 century DR.

Activities
He was a pirate.

Relationships
Petrove was great-uncle to Coprates Bile, and told her many tales of the sea.

History
The tales Petrove told as great-uncle to Coprates Bile were formative in her ambitions to become a pirate captain, and were an important part of what made her sneak out of her home at the tender age of 13 years, approximately in the, to become a cabin boy.

He was slain later around, by sahuagin, which made her hate them intensely and forever, never missing the opportunity to kill one.

## Pe're

Pe're was a sea elf sergeant in Myth Nantar during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Abilities
His abilities were level with those of other sea elven sergeants.

Possessions
He carried a trident and a conch he could blow on for reinforcements.

Activities
Pe're held the rank of sergeant in Myth Nantar.

History
When in, Sturge Whitsunday was sent to find Myth Nantar by the mayor of Selgaunt, Andeth Ilchammar, if Sturge got far enough, his first contact with the mystical city would be when Pe're captured him for trespassing into the city's territories.

Inside the city, his crew would be interrogated, and then brought to trial. Pe're would argue for their execution if they'd attacked any of his men.
